### Slide 112

**Text**

There will be many more TikToks / The US can no longer assume that every hot new thing will be made in the US / Venture capital investments ($bn) / Rest of World / US / 350 / 300 / 250 / 200 / 150 / 100 / 50 / 0 / 2004 / 2005 / 2006 / 2007 / 2008 / 2009 / 2010 / 2011 / 2012 / 2013 / 2014 / 2015 / 2016 / 2017 / 2018 / 2019 / Source: NVCA / Benedict Evans — February 2020 / 112

**Layout**

Single column with a full-width grey header band (top about 0 to 20.5 percent) holding the title and subtitle left-aligned at x 20.1 percent, a white body band (about 20.5 to 86 percent) holding one chart whose left edge is at about x 20 percent, and a grey footer band (about 86 to 100 percent) holding the source line at left and the author, date and page number at right. Legend sits at the far left at x 3 to 12 percent, aligned with the upper chart area; the chart title sits above the plot at x 20.5 percent. Left and right margins are about 20 percent and 4 percent respectively, and the page number 112 is right-aligned at about x 95.5 percent.

**Visuals**

One stacked vertical bar chart titled 'Venture capital investments ($bn)' in bold sans-serif. Y axis: US dollars in billions, range 0 to 350, gridlines and tick labels at 0, 50, 100, 150, 200, 250, 300, 350. X axis: years 2004 to 2019, one category per year, no axis title. Two stacked series per bar: US (grey, bottom segment) and Rest of World (red, top segment). No values are printed on the bars; estimates: 2004 total about 26 (US about 22, RoW about 4); 2005 total about 30 (US about 24); 2006 total about 37 (US about 30); 2007 total about 46 (US about 37); 2008 total about 50 (US about 37); 2009 total about 36 (US about 27); 2010 total about 47 (US about 32); 2011 total about 65 (US about 45); 2012 total about 61 (US about 41); 2013 total about 72 (US about 48); 2014 total about 114 (US about 71); 2015 total about 151 (US about 80); 2016 total about 159 (US about 77); 2017 total about 183 (US about 86); 2018 total about 309 (US about 142); 2019 total about 257 (US about 134). Legend at upper left, top entry Rest of World with red square, second entry US with grey square. No highlighted points. Bars are narrow, separated by white hairline gaps; gridlines are light dotted grey horizontal lines.

**Design**

Background: Light grey #F1F1F1 covering the whole slide, with a white #FFFFFF band behind the chart area. Off-white and white two-tone layout with restrained grey palette. Title in a light regular high-contrast serif (Photina-style) at about 5.6 percent of slide height, colour #545454. Subtitle in the same serif, about 2.6 percent of slide height, colour #545454. Chart title in bold Helvetica Neue style sans-serif, about 2.2 percent, colour #545554. Axis labels, legend and source in Helvetica Neue regular, about 1.85 percent, colours #545454 and #535353. Footer author name in Helvetica Neue bold, date and page number in Helvetica Neue regular, colour #A6A6A6. Accent colour red #EA4A3C for Rest of World bars and legend swatch; US bars and legend swatch grey #808080. Gridlines dotted, thin, mid grey #A0A0A0. Shapes are flat with square corners, no shadows, no gradients.

### Slide 113

**Text**

From sovereignty (or protectionism) to extraterritoriality
Again, how do local rules apply to global systems?
Old
Store your data in / our country
New
Follow our rules in / our country
Future
Use our country’s / rules outside our / country
Benedict Evans — February 2020
113

Three filled circles of equal size in a left-to-right sequence connected by two right-pointing block arrows. Between circles 1 and 2 and between circles 2 and 3 sit two solid light-grey #A6A6A6 right-pointing block arrows (chevron-tailed block arrow shape, flat, no outline). No chart, table, photo or logo.

### Slide 114

**Text**

The future – compliance and moderation as a service?
GCP/AWS/Azure all now rolling this out (will Facebook join them?)
Microsoft Azure | Content Moderator / ... / Sign In
Content Moderator
Sign up for the Content Mod[obscured] moderation with human rev[obscured] your colleagues, and config[obscured]
Sign Up
Machine - Assisted Moderation
aws / Contact Sales / Support / English / My Account / Create an AWS Account
Products / Solutions / Pricing / Documentation / Learn / Partner Network / AWS Marketplace / Custom
Enhanced Content Moderation is now available for Amazon Rekognition Video
Posted On: Feb 13, 2019
Amazon Rekognition Video is a deep learning-based video analysis service that can identify objects, people, text, scenes, and activities, as well as detect unsafe content. Content Moderation for Amazon Rekognition Video can identify various types of explicit and suggestive content, as well as timestamps for where such content appears during the video. Amazon Rekognition Video now comes with an enhanced moderation model that reduces false positive rates by 40% on average without any reduction in detection rates for truly unsafe content. Lower false positive rates imply lower volumes of flagged videos to be further reviewed by human moderators, leading to higher efficiency and more cost savings. Moreover, Amazon Rekognition provides a hierarchical set of moderation labels that can be used to create business rules to handle different geographic and demographic requirements. For more details on supported labels, please see this page. No machine learning experience is required to get started.
Google Cloud / Sign in / AI & Machine Learning Products / Contact sales / Get started for free
AI & Machine Learning Products > Cloud Vision API > Documentation > Guides
Detect explicit content (Safe Search)
Contents
Safe Search detection requests
Set up your GCP project and authentication
Explicit content detection on a local image
Explicit content detection on a remote image
Note: We've recently added new features or fields to Safe Search Detection. See the release notes for details.
Safe Search Detection detects explicit content such as adult content or violent content within an image. This feature uses five categories (adult, spoof, medical, violence, and racy) and returns the likelihood that each is present in a given image. See the SafeSearchAnnotation page for details on these fields.
Benedict Evans
–– February 2020

### Slide 115

**Text**

But what will happen when regulators conflict? / When local regulators assert global coverage and make incompatible demands, who yields first? / Police access / Open platforms / Catch state ‘bad actors’ / Secure, private, minimal data collection / Trustworthy information / Empower competitors / Moderate ‘bad’ content / Benedict Evans –– February 2020 / 115

**Layout**

Centred hub-and-spoke diagram on a plain light grey field. The title is left-aligned at about x 20 %, y 5.5 %, with a one-line subtitle directly beneath it at y 13 %, both sharing the same left edge. Six satellite circles surround one large central circle, arranged in a ring and roughly centred on the slide. The footer sits at the lower right, around y 91 %: 'Benedict Evans –– February 2020' followed by the page number 115 at x 93.8 %, with no logo and no rule. Margins are wide and the slide has no visible grid lines.

**Visuals**

Diagram: one large central circle (#7F7F7F, flat, no outline) labelled 'Secure, private, minimal data collection'. Six smaller circles of the same grey (#7F7F7F, flat, no outline) sit around it, labelled clockwise from top-left: 'Police access' (upper left), 'Open platforms' (upper right), 'Trustworthy information' (right), 'Moderate ‘bad’ content' (lower right), 'Empower competitors' (lower left), and 'Catch state ‘bad actors’' (left). Each satellite is joined to the centre by a red double-headed arrow (about #EE4B3F, stroke about 0.6 % of slide height, filled triangular arrowheads at both ends) that runs from the satellite's edge to the centre circle's edge. The arrows are short straight diagonal or horizontal segments with no labels. No photographs, logos or icons.

**Design**

Background is flat light grey #F1F1F1 with no gradient or texture. Title and subtitle are in a serif (Photina-style, regular weight) in dark grey #545454; the title is about 5.6 % of slide height and the subtitle about 2.6 %. Circle labels are white (#FFFFFF) serif regular at about 3.0 %, centred, with tight line spacing. Footer is sans-serif (Helvetica Neue): 'Benedict Evans' bold, the rest regular, in light grey #A6A6A6 at about 1.9 %. Circles are flat #7F7F7F with no shadows, and the arrows are the only accent colour (red #EE4B3F). No corner radii apply; circles are perfect circles.

### Slide 116

**Text**

And this may get even more complicated
Part of the point of crypto is to remove central control – and hence sovereignty
Old
Mark Zuckerberg / won’t come to / Parliament
New
Will the CEO of / Tiktok come to / Congress?
Next
A distributed / Blockchain system / that has no CEO?
Benedict Evans — February 2020 116

**Layout**

Free-form slide with no grid or columns: title and subtitle are left-aligned at x≈20% on a shared left edge, and three equal circles sit in a horizontal row across the middle of the slide (x≈19% to 96%) with two small arrows between them. Footer credit and slide number sit bottom-right at y≈91%, with the slide number at the right margin near x≈94%.

**Visuals**

Three filled circles in a left-to-right flow, connected by two right-pointing block arrows. Circle 1 (grey #7F7F7F) labelled 'Old' / 'Mark Zuckerberg won't come to Parliament'. Circle 2 (grey #7F7F7F) labelled 'New' / 'Will the CEO of Tiktok come to Congress?'. Circle 3 (red #EB483F) labelled 'Next' / 'A distributed Blockchain system that has no CEO?'. Each circle is about 22.1% of slide width and 39.3% of slide height, with the label centred in the circle, white serif text. Two block arrows (#BFBFBF, flat, no outline), each about 4.0% wide and 10.4% tall, positioned in the gaps between circles, pointing right. No photographs, charts, or logos. No shadows or gradients.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1. Plain light grey background #F1F1F1, no gradient or texture. Title in a serif face (PhotinaMT-like, regular weight), colour #545454, about 5.6% of slide height. Subtitle in the same serif, #545454, about 2.6% of slide height. Circle labels in the same serif, white #FFFFFF, about 3.0% of slide height, with the heading 'Old/New/Next' on its own line and body text below in centred lines. Footer in Helvetica Neue, #A6A6A6, about 1.9% of slide height, with 'Benedict Evans' bold. Accent colour red #EB483F used only on the third circle. Shapes are flat, with no outlines, shadows, or corner rounding beyond the circle geometry.

### Slide 117

**Text**

The only certainty: regulation as a regressive tax
Much of regulation is a fixed cost that affects new entrants disproportionately
Mean compliance costs at US financial institutions by size
10.0%
7.5%
5.0%
2.5%
0.0%
<$100m
$100-250m
$250-500m
$500m-1bn
$1bn-10bn
Source: Dahl, Meyer and Neely 2016
Benedict Evans
—— February 2020
117

**Layout**

Full-bleed light grey (#F1F1F1) header band across the top with the title and subtitle left-aligned at x about 20.1%, a white middle band holding a single bar chart spanning roughly x 17% to 95%, and a light grey footer band below it. The source line sits at the left margin (x 20.1%) and the author, date and slide number sit right-aligned at the bottom right, with the slide number at x 93.8%.

**Visuals**

Vertical bar chart of mean compliance costs at US financial institutions by size. Chart title: 'Mean compliance costs at US financial institutions by size'. Y-axis: percentage of costs, labelled 0.0% to 10.0% in 2.5% steps, gridlines dotted grey at each step, range 0 to 10%. X-axis: institution size bands in US dollars: <$100m, $100-250m, $250-500m, $500m-1bn, $1bn-10bn. Single series, all bars red (#EC473F). Values are not printed; estimated values: <$100m about 8.6%, $100-250m about 6.0%, $250-500m about 5.3%, $500m-1bn about 4.0%, $1bn-10bn about 2.9%. No legend. No highlighted points; all bars same colour. Bars are solid, flat, with no outlines.

**Design**

Background: Light grey #F1F1F1 full-slide fill with a white #FFFFFF band from about y 20.5% to 86.1%. Light grey (#F1F1F1) background with a white central band for the chart. Title in a serif face resembling Photina, regular weight, dark grey #545454, large (about 5.6% of slide height). Subtitle in the same serif, regular, #545454, about 2.6% of height. Chart title in bold sans-serif Helvetica Neue Bold, #545554, about 2.2% of height. Axis labels and source in Helvetica Neue regular, #545454, about 1.85% of height. Footer author name in Helvetica Neue Bold #A6A6A6, date and slide number in Helvetica Neue regular #A6A6A6, about 1.85% of height. Bars red #EC473F, square corners, no shadows or gradients. Gridlines thin dotted light grey.

Five S-shaped curved arrows, one per column, stepping upward from lower-left to upper-right. ### Slide 121

**Text**

Worrying about tech isn’t new
The late 70s and early 80s saw another wave of concern around computing and automation
The Politics of Privacy, Computers, and Criminal Justice Records
DONALD A. MARCHAND
THE RISE OF THE COMPUTER STATE
Foreword by
WALTER
CRONKITE
THE THREAT TO OUR FREEDOMS, OUR ETHICS AND OUR DEMOCRATIC PROCESS
DAVID BURNHAM
Databanks in a Free Society
Computers, Record Keeping and Privacy
Librairie du Bassin
JOHN WICKLEIN
ELECTRONIC NIGHTMARE
THE NEW COMMUNICATIONS AND FREEDOM
Benedict Evans –– February 2020
121

**Layout**

Four book-cover images sit in a single horizontal row across the lower-middle of the slide, left-aligned to a title block that starts at about 20% from the left edge. The title and subtitle are stacked at the top-left, and the footer line (author, date and page number) sits at the bottom right on one baseline, with the page number at the far right margin.

**Visuals**

Four photographs of book covers, shown as flat front-cover scans with no overlay, shadow or border, arranged left to right. (1) Far left: The Politics of Privacy, Computers, and Criminal Justice Records by Donald A. Marchand, a tan/beige cloth-textured cover with dark serif title text and two horizontal rules; portrait, full cover visible. (2) Second: The Rise of the Computer State, foreword by Walter Cronkite, by David Burnham, a white cover with bold condensed grey-black capitals, red sans subtitle text, and a black-and-white cartoon of a face-like computer with a small figure at a terminal. (3) Third: Databanks in a Free Society, Computers, Record Keeping and Privacy, a grey cover with a faint concentric-rectangle pattern and a pale green rectangle with a small grey image, and blue text reading Librairie du Bassin at lower right. (4) Far right: Electronic Nightmare by John Wicklein, a black cover with red and white condensed capitals, the word NIGHTMARE with a red-to-yellow-to-white gradient, and white bold caps for The New Communications and Freedom. Covers are of similar portrait proportions, not cropped, and differ slightly in pixel size.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1 covering the whole slide. Plain flat design. Background light grey #F1F1F1. Title and subtitle are in a book-style serif (Photina-like) in dark grey #545454. Footer uses a Helvetica Neue-style sans-serif, bold for the author name and regular for the date and page number, in light grey #A6A6A6. Title is about 5.6% of slide height, subtitle and body about 2.6%, footer about 1.9%. No accent colours beyond the book covers. Photos have square corners, no shadows, no borders.

The footer, a credit line with the author name in bold followed by the date, and the page number sit bottom-right at about y 91%, the page number right-aligned to about 95%.

Diagram: a left-to-right linear flow of four solid circles connected by three light grey block arrows. Circle 1 (grey #7F7F7F) at x 20.2%, y 41.3%, 13.8% by 24.5%, labelled Railways. ### Slide 124

**Text**

And this also needs new understanding in policy…
Regulating tech is complex and full of tradeoffs. But, this is true of all regulation – law and policy are the art of the possible
Some regulation
is binary
Ban tobacco ads
Open banking APIs
Most is ‘best
reasonable effort’
Try to prevent fraud
Try to block CSAM
Some things are
just not possible
Ban inflation
Block all bad content
instantly
Benedict Evans
— February 2020
124

**Layout**

Single slide with a left-aligned title and subtitle starting at about 20% from the left edge, running across the top. Title is about 5.6% of slide height, subtitle about 2.6%, circle headings about 3.3%, circle sub-lines about 3.0%, footer about 1.9%. The growth chart for the 2020s
Welcome to a regulated industry

30,000
20,000
10,000
0
Year 1
Year 2
Year 3
Year 4
Year 5
Source: Davis Polk & Wardell LLP
Benedict Evans –– February 2020
125

**Layout**

Full-bleed light grey (#F1F1F1) frame with a white central band spanning y 20.5% to 86.1%, leaving grey header (top 20.5%) and footer (bottom 13.9%) bands. Title and subtitle are left-aligned at x 20.1% in the header; the chart runs from x about 16.5% to 95.3% inside the white band, with the chart title left-aligned at x 20.5%. Source line sits left at x 20.1% in the footer; author, date and page number sit right-hand in the footer, with the page number at x 93.8%, ending near the 95% right margin.

**Visuals**

Chart: vertical stacked bar chart, title 'Pages of rules related to Dodd-Frank banking regulation'. Y axis is pages, 0 to 30,000 (gridlines and labels at 0, 10,000, 20,000, 30,000; no unit label printed). X axis categories are Year 1, Year 2, Year 3, Year 4, Year 5. Bar totals (about, no values printed): Year 1 about 4,000; Year 2 about 9,000; Year 3 about 14,100; Year 4 about 19,900; Year 5 about 22,200. Each bar has a lighter divider line inside it marking a second stacked segment, at about 3,300 (Year 1), 4,800 (Year 2), 7,000 (Year 3), 8,500 (Year 4) and 9,500 (Year 5), all estimates. No legend, no data labels, no highlighted points. All bars are a single red #EB483F with no outline. Horizontal gridlines are dotted grey at 10,000, 20,000 and 30,000; the baseline at 0 is a solid thin grey line. Bar width is about 6% of slide width, evenly spaced across the plot.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1 covering the whole slide, with a white #FFFFFF band from y 20.5% to 86.1%. Light grey #F1F1F1 header and footer bands with a pure white #FFFFFF central band. Title in Photina-style high-contrast serif, regular weight, colour #545454, about 5.6% of slide height. Subtitle in the same serif, regular, #545454, about 2.6% of slide height. Chart title and axis labels in Helvetica Neue sans-serif; chart title bold, #545454, about 2.2%; axis labels regular, #545454, about 1.85%. Footer source in Helvetica Neue regular #545454, about 1.85%. Author name in Helvetica Neue bold #A6A6A6, date and page number in Helvetica Neue regular #A6A6A6, about 1.85%. Accent colour is red #EB483F for bars only. Bars are flat with square corners, no shadows, no gradients. Gridlines are 1px dotted grey, and the baseline is a 1px solid grey line.

### Slide 126

**Text**

Thank you
Benedict Evans
February 2020
www.ben-evans.com

**Layout**

Single full-bleed red slide with a translucent white panel occupying the right and lower area (x 17.3 to 95.4 percent, y 20.4 to 81.5 percent) and a thin white rule along the panel's bottom edge. Title sits top-left inside the panel, with the presenter block bottom-left inside the panel; all text is left-aligned on a shared left edge at about x 20.2 percent. No footer, page number or logo is visible; a small footer line is present in the file but is covered by a red rectangle at bottom right.

**Visuals**

none

**Design**

Background: Solid red #EB483F full-bleed fill. Flat red background #EB483F with no gradient or pattern. A white panel at 4 percent opacity sits on top, giving a barely lighter red field, with square corners and no shadow. A 1-pixel-scale white horizontal rule runs along the bottom edge of the panel. Title in a light high-contrast serif (PhotinaMT, regular weight) at about 9.26 percent of slide height, white #FFFFFF. Presenter name in HelveticaNeue Bold at about 2.59 percent of slide height, white #FFFFFF; date and URL in HelveticaNeue regular at the same size, white #FFFFFF. Accent colour is the same red #EB483F; the hidden footer text is #121212.
